The verify line is gone: the owner asked whether it is needed; it opens the same card on the same site, so it proves nothing (checking our record on our own site is circular); the independent proof would be the calls' X post, timestamped by X before the report. Slim dates (one line each), rows 14px, the market 19px; nothing overflows at 360 and 390px; the digital stamp steps down to 84px below 380px. **"Market" for "Outcome" on X2+ (owner, same day: "a change we have to do across the entire website"):** build.py's X2+ patch list sets `ACTUAL_WORD` to "Market" (the record back's close row, everywhere bkOut draws it); v15 and the locked pages keep Outcome. On both artifacts and both previews since 2026-10-25; live on earningszn.xyz since 2026-10-25 with the R44 film v2.
- **Round 44 mockups, our own EZ stamp (owner, 2026-10-25, on round 43: the stamps "need to be integrated... can't just be slapped on top"; a proprietary "EZ stamp of approval", "not dollar sign easy, just easy"; waiting for the owner):** `ez/x2p-launch-r44-ez-stamps.png` (390px) and `-closeup.png` (4x), files `launch-r40-recback/n1..n6.js` through `R44` (end of `_rows.js`; CSS `.x44` at the end of `_base.css`), on round 43's card (Certificate, gold line, hallmark PASS/FAIL, slim dates, Market). The EZ mark: a monogram whose E and Z share the top and bottom bars (82 x 60 units, `R44.mono`). Integrated means drawn the way the real thing marks paper: ink with speckle where the rubber missed, a slow pressure change across the strike and a 0.3-unit bleed, multiplied into the stock and struck over the frame's corner (`ink()` filter); or the paper itself changed. 1 ink seal (red, rim EZ GRADED / BY THE MARKET CLOSE, the date), 2 two inks (E green, Z red, two blocks a hair out of register, GRADED + date beside), 3 embosser (a notary's crimp seal, no ink, only the paper's light and shadow, masked so the face stays paper), 4 perfin (EZ punched in holes, the cancelled-cheque homage, the date in red ink), 5 red foil debossed (a dark wall inside the top edge, a light lip inside the bottom), 6 wax pressed (thin edge, an oil halo; wax always sits on the paper). Nothing overflows the card's content at 360 and 390px (the stamps cross the frame on purpose). Filters are live per card here; a build should draw each stamp once as an image (WebKit has no GPU path for SVG filters).
- **Round 49 mockups, the stamp as a hologram (owner, 2026-10-25, on round 48: back to round 47's card; the winner's golden line stays, its mark PASS in gold instead of WINNER; PASS and FAIL smaller "so it gives more room for the model name, the prediction"; Calls filed at the foot beside the stamp, a bit higher than just above See the film, "maybe not even have a module around it"; the stamp a new way in colour and design: a hologram like the $EZ one on every card's label, "like passports... they have these holos"; waiting for the owner):** `ez/x2p-launch-r49-holo.png` (390px; 7 keeps the box round Calls filed), `-closeup.png` (4x) and `-holo.mp4` (the six foils moving, a 6 s loop shot frame by frame); files `ez/tools/concepts/launch-r40-recback/_r49.js` (`R49`), `r49*.js`, CSS at the end of `_base.css`, and `ez/tools/concepts/launch-r49-holo.js` (the clip). The card: round 47's (Certificate between gold stars, No., What they predicted), the winner's row in the satin gold with a gold PASS, the marks 29x13px (were 36x16), the name column 58px and the rows 15px, Calls filed stacked without its box, 20px above See the film. Six holograms, each carrying the EZ. logo: 1 holo certificate seal (round 47's scalloped seal in a silver rainbow foil, the avatar at its heart, GRADED and the date on the rim), 2 the label's hologram grown (the same mint, cream, rose and gold foil as the label's $EZ), 3 passport rosette (guilloche curves in a cool foil, the EZ. demetallised so the card shows through, a microtext rim), 4 the logo itself (the X avatar as a die-cut sticker, its black disc an oil-slick foil), 5 ink over holo (a visa-style laminate with the EZ. running through it, GRADED struck across in green ink), 6 authentication hologram (iridescent gold, the avatar, GRADED, the date, the serial EZ-0043 and a three-letter code from the first bytes of the SHA-256 of the round's record, after MLB's authentication stickers). The foils drift on one 6 s clock (SMIL animateTransform on each gradient, with a passing glint; the boards hold t = 1.6 s). Research: passport holograms are DOVIDs, diffractive foils whose colours, images and microtext change with the angle, built into the page or hot-stamped on paper (OVD Kinegram's own pages, a vendor's); MLB's authentication sticker is a tamper-evident hologram with a unique prefix and serial, a three-letter validation code and a QR code, each logged in a public database (mlb.com). For a build: one hologram per card back (field tests: more than two seals lowered conversion); only the card in front should animate (the SPEED rules already hold the others still); the SMIL drift repaints the stamp every frame while it runs.
- **Round 50 mockups, the holograms refined (owner, 2026-10-25, on round 49: the foot reads Calls filed, then Graded on, then "boom, the stamp", and the stamp carries no date; See the film smaller; liked the seal ("a bit more integrated"), the label's hologram ("turn that one a little bit more to the right and higher up"), the passport rosette ("a bit more subtle"), the logo itself ("more stampy... the logo doesn't have to be so big... the letters themselves can be holo"), the authentication hologram ("the same color as... the holo certificate seal and not... the golden one. Or have a sort of the calls filed box"); and above all "the logo should be part of the stamp... right now it feels like the holo stamps have the logo on top of them... it could just be the logo EZ, but in the holo style", since the full logo will be across the site; the ink-over-holo was not mentioned and is dropped; waiting for the owner):** `ez/x2p-launch-r50-holo.png` (390px), `-closeup.png` (4x) and `-holo.mp4` (6 s loop); files `ez/tools/concepts/launch-r40-recback/_r50.js` (`R50`, reusing R49's helpers, which R49 now exports), `r50*.js`, CSS at the end of `_base.css`. The foot: "Calls filed / Oct 22" and "Graded on / Oct 23" side by side, See the film under them at 8.4px (was 10px), the stamp on the right, pressed flush (a dark wall above, a pale lip below, no lift). The logo in the foil (R50.mark): the EZ. shape itself in the hologram, the letters a second, broader foil drifting the other way from the field's, the slashes and the dot in green, red and gold tinted foils, a raised edge (light above and left, dark below and right) and a fine keyline; never the black-disc avatar. 1 seal, hot-stamped (the card's grain pressed through the foil), 2 the label's hologram turned 9 degrees right and 8px higher, 3 the rosette paler with the whole EZ. cut out of the foil, 4 the logo stamp (the oil-slick disc with two foil rings and GRADED BY THE MARKET CLOSE round it, the EZ. smaller and in foil), 5 the authentication hologram in the seal's silver rainbow, 6 the same hologram holding Calls filed, Graded on and the serial (my reading of "or have a sort of the calls filed box"; then no dates beside it). Measured: nothing overflows at 360 and 390px.
- **The certificate on the record (owner, 2026-10-25: "do the logo stamp. Put that one on the Easy Life X2 Plus, and then put the seal hot stamped on Easy Life X3 Plus"; on both artifacts and both previews, production waits for the owner's go):** every graded record card's stats side is round 50's certificate (x2plus.js `certify()`, the CERT block at the end of skin-x2plus.css; board `ez/x2p-cert-live.png`): ★ Certificate ★ and No. 00nn; What they predicted, ranked 1 to 4 (right calls by closeness, then wrong ones), PASS or FAIL in small worn cartouches (four shared filters `#x2pcw0` to 3 in one hidden svg), the winner's line in antique gold with a gold PASS (a tie: both lines); Market; Calls filed and Graded on at the foot beside the stamp (no date in it), the page's own flip button printed small under them ("See the film" or "See the picture"; none on a no-film round). The stamp: the logo stamp on X2+ (R50.badge: the oil-slick disc, two foil rings, GRADED · BY THE MARKET CLOSE, the logo in foil), the certificate seal hot-stamped on X3+ (R50.seal); build.py writes `window.__x2pStampKind="seal"` into X3+ only. The label's back line reads "2 of 4 right" in Anton with "· 7.19% off on avg" smaller after it (rounds 40 and 41); where it would run under the grade, " on avg" goes on a 351 to 385px phone and the miss goes at 350px and below (media queries: the label is 58vw - 24px wide on phones). Drawn for the 390px card (stats side 202 x 290) and scaled in CSS: every length is a multiple of `--u`, viewport units only (below 440px the stats side's width, 58vw - 24px, over 202; from 440 to 759px its height, 58vw + 57px, over 290, since the card there is shorter than the drawing; from 760px 306px over 202): .80 at 320px, .91 at 360, 1 at 390, 1.12 at 430, 1.08 at 440, 1.71 at 759, 1.51 from 760. Two earlier ways were measured and dropped before shipping: zooming by script and measuring the labels forced whole-page layouts at load (6.9 s and 3.5 s on a 4x slower CPU), and container units (cqw/cqh on 45 stats sides) took the cover from about 14 s to 46 s there. Keep this block free of script measuring and container units. The name column gives up to 14 design px when a call is wide (▲2.25% is 55 of a 182px row). Speed: certificates and stamps are drawn only for the front card and two either side (built for all 45 at load they cost the first build about 1 s on a 4x slower CPU), and for the round opened over the page (its copy gets fresh ids, `__x2pCertCopy`); the page's own stats side stays under the certificate, hidden and still, so a card's height never depends on how many carry one. The foil drifts only on the front card with its stats side up while the record is on screen, or on the opened round when turned (`xlive`, a MutationObserver on #car and #record), never with reduced motion, stepped by script 20 times a second on a paused SMIL clock (`xstep`: SMIL's own 60 updates cost 2.1 s of work in 4 s at 4x and dropped the page to 35 fps; 20 steps cost 0.8 to 1.0 s and keep 48); every other stamp holds the boards' frame (t = 1.6 s). Card heights: from 351px up the front sets them (`contain:size` on the back), 379px at 360 (was 404.5) and 400 at 390 (404.5); the highlights take that height and still fit (checked at 352, 360, 390); at 350px and below the old height stays (435.5 at 320, the MVP stories need it) and up to 332px the rows are set looser. Load, interleaved medians on a 4x slower CPU at 390px: X2+ 14.8 s against 14.2 s for the build before (6 runs each), X3+ 15.6 against 15.2: within run-to-run noise (single runs ranged 13.1 to 15.6 s). Checked at 320 to 1920px (27 widths, every certificate forced) on all 45 graded cards: no row, box or label line overflows, no errors, no duplicate ids. Not checked: Safari (background-clip:text with filter, SMIL). Left as on the boards, asked: the rule under "What they predicted" runs through the gold frame on both sides.
- **The EZ. logo across the site (owner, 2026-10-25: the X avatar "needs to be the logo across the site... I don't think like $EZ needs to be everywhere"; $EZ in the films is fine; "one thing that we still have to think about"; not built):** traced to a vector, `ez/tools/concepts/ez-logo-traced.svg` (a 400 box: black disc #04070e, cream letters #f8f0d9, green #03c870 and red #ff3a31 slashes, a gold dot #fdbc2a), cleaned: in the avatar the cut between the E's top bar and the Z ends in a small stair-step (owner: "is that a visual issue there?"; yes), now a clean point, and the tracing's sub-pixel corner bevels are squared (`ez/x2p-logo-step-fix.png`). Where $EZ works as a logo on X2+ today: the header mark (`.brand .mark`, $ gold, E green, Z red, beside "Earnings Zeason"), the loading cover (`tools/v22/x2p-mark.svg`), the slab label's hologram and its "2026 $EZ SEASON 1" line, the film bill's "$EZ presents". The page has no favicon and no share image (make_site.py writes og:title "$EZ · Earnings Zeason" and twitter:card summary_large_image but no og:image or twitter:image, so a link to the site posted on X shows no picture). Copy that names the coin ("69% of $EZ creator fees", "Get $EZ", "$EZ buyback") stays $EZ. **Owner, same day:** the logo everywhere ("that's going to be our logo. It's not like Nike has a different logo at other places"); asked whether the letters should go green E and red Z, whether it needs 4K and what ratio, and what to ask the ChatGPT session that made it. `ez/x2p-logo-colours.png` (`ez/tools/concepts/logo-colours.js`, the green and red version `ez/tools/concepts/ez-logo-greenred.svg`): on the black disc the cream letters are 17.7:1, a green E 9.1:1 and a red Z 5.7:1 (WCAG luminance contrast); green letters run into the green slashes and red into the red, so the slashes stop reading at 32px and below; for red-green colour blindness (deuteranopia, Machado et al. 2009) the green and red letters turn into two muddy olives while the cream stays crisp; recommended cream as the master (green and red already live in the slashes). The E and the Z are one outline, joined only where the E's top bar meets the Z's; the Z is everything right of the cut and of its own left edges. No 4K needed: the site draws the logo from the vector at any size; from ChatGPT, at most a square (1:1) PNG on a transparent background at its largest native size (no upscaling), with and without the black disc, to check details against.

- **Live, round 2, the on-air dot (owner, 2026-10-26; waiting for the owner):** `ez/x2p-live-r2-pill.png` and `ez/x2p-live-r2-page.png`, files `ez/tools/concepts/live-r2.js` and `live-r2/` (reuses live-r1's model; `_state.js` and `_r2.css` are shared). The dot is honest: red with a breathing halo only while something is live (calls due today, or rounds graded at today's close while the market is open), amber and slow after the bell until the results are synced, grey and still off air (weekends, nothing on); still with reduced motion. Pills: F today's yellow pill with the honest dot and true words ("8 due", "2h 51m", "Results", "Back Mon"), G an ON AIR studio lightbox, H a red LIVE broadcast bug, I a tally lamp with a clock ticking every second to 4pm New York, J a ticket stub with the round numbers on now. P4b: the week strip with the dot on today's stub and the state in its head, the pill (F there) jumping to it. Every shot measured at 320, 390 and 1280px: no sideways scroll, no overlap with the logo, the X or the tabs (H's skewed tab overruns its own box by 2px, invisible). "Live" on a calls day means the calls are due, not that they are in.

- **MVP backs, money (owner, 2026-10-26: the four call chips at the bottom of the MVP story side replaced by "how much money it made on the call or lost"; the live/season/headline rounds 4 and 5 are paused until this is done; mockups, waiting for the owner):** `ez/x2p-mvp-money.png` (390px) and `-desktop.png`, files `ez/tools/concepts/mvp-money.js` (`node mvp-money.js now,A,B,C,D`, `WS=375,412` for other widths) and `mvp-money/` (`money.js` replaces `.x2p-hcs` in every highlight card; `money.css`; `_board.py`): A $1,000 per call at the bet's leverage (SIM.leverage, 5x) in today's 2x2 (Tesla ±$154, TSMC +$100 each, Accenture −$801 each), B the same as four rows, C $1,000 per call with no leverage (the ROI tab's rules; ±$30.80 on Tesla), D each AI's running $100K pot from the ROI tab (Tesla: Grok +$4,177, Claude +$3,505, GPT −$3,410, Kimi −$2,362, with the pot after). Money follows direction only, so every AI that got the direction right makes the same %: the bullseye earns what a call 1.4% off earns. At 5x a 16.02% move against loses 80%; it is not a liquidation (Hyperliquid closes a 5x trade out at about a 17.5 to 20% move). The block is taller than the chips, so the page's fitting sets the story smaller on phones (10 to 11px at 360 and 390, was 12.5). Mockup classes carry an mm prefix (the site's `.u`/`.d` drew red and green boxes behind the calls). The owner's phone showed the analogy running under the call chips on R40; not reproduced in Chromium at 360 to 430px (no overlap measured), so likely Safari's text metrics or a fit before the fonts loaded.

Graded at the close." / "Top accuracy makes the buyback call."). Proof: tabs P&L, Accuracy, Avg miss, Sector; P&L rows Biggest W / Biggest L / P&L (Claude +$13.6K BE / −$13.9K CCL; Grok +$13.1K LRCX / −$16.5K ACN; GPT +$14.7K CCL / −$20.3K ACN; Kimi +$14.2K CCL / −$19.7K ACN), the chart's line ends print the P&L % in place of the model; Sector: the best right/made ratio per row crowned (ties share; none when all four tie, Materials), gold cells or crowns with the rest dimmed. Flagged: the owner reads the ROI tab as "$100K into every trade"; it is one account that lets wins ride (a flat $100K per call gives Claude +$43,320, Grok +$37,270, GPT +$25,780, Kimi −$33,080); the board ranks by accuracy (Grok #1 makes the buyback call) while Claude leads P&L; "W" now means right direction while a round's "winner" is the closest call; TSMC's winner Claude made less than GPT on that call (bigger account); Accenture is the biggest L for three of four AIs.

Sizing by the predicted move would have lowered every leader's P&L against a flat book of the same average size (S1: Claude +$20,858 vs +$30,308 flat, Grok +$8,404 vs +$17,942, GPT +$1,198 vs +$11,586; only Kimi gains, −$6,187 vs −$18,350): the AIs' big calls are right about as often as their small ones (Claude 14/23 vs 15/22, Grok 13/19 vs 12/18, GPT 12/23 vs 13/22, Kimi 13/23 vs 8/22), so the predicted size is not yet a conviction signal; recommended: keep direction-only now and ask each AI to state a position size from the next calls on, so sizing becomes a recorded skill. `ez/x2p-pnl-avgmiss.png` (`pnl2.js miss`, `pnl2/miss.js`, `miss.css`), Avg miss in the competition voice: M1 round wins (the sheet's own winner column, a tie counts for both: Claude 15, GPT 13, Grok 7, Kimi 6, as gold belts), M2 target hits (every call as a bar: within 1%, 1 to 3%, over 3%, wrong way; Grok 12 of 37, GPT 10 of 45, Claude 9, Kimi 4 within 1%), M3 called vs moved (each AI's calls against the real move, the diagonal a perfect call), M4 the targets kept with Bullseyes / Within 1% / Off when right under each. All fit the ticket at 360 and 390px. The Avg miss tab is **"Target hits"**: no average miss on the captions; under each target its Bullseyes (right way, within 0.1%), Within 1% (bullseyes included) and Off target (the wrong way, the red dots outside, in red ink) (`.pt-ts`); ranked by bullseyes, then within 1%, then fewer off target, so GPT is #1 (2 bullseyes, 10 within 1%, 20 off target) over Grok (1, 12, 12), Claude (0, 9, 16), Kimi (0, 4, 24); the note reads "The bullseye is what the stock did. Checked at 320, 360, 390 and 1280px: nothing overflows, no errors. **Then (owner, same day, from a sketch):** the Accuracy rows are Trades · W · L · Accuracy (the hit rate back as the last column, the biggest figure, #1's in gold ink), and the line ends carry the model names again (Grok 4.7 ...); Target hits has no red dots outside the targets ("too much noise"; Off target counts them), the counts in colour: bullseyes gold, within 1% green, off target red; its note ends "Off target: the wrong way." **Then (owner, same day, a second sketch):** the Accuracy columns packed to the right with the gap from Trades to W equal to the gap from L to Accuracy (measured between the figures: 34/35px at 390px, 24/25 at 320 to 360, 52/54 at 1280) and W and L closer (20px at 390); the Target hits counts in gold, green and red (the proof's print rule had kept them in ink; the selector now repeats its class to outrank it). **Labels aligned (owner, same day: "the W is not aligned"):** the header and each row had sized their own auto columns (W 12.6px off at 390px); the Accuracy list is now one grid and the header and rows take its columns (`grid-template-columns:subgrid`): every label within 0.7px of its figures at 320 to 1280px; the Trades column is as wide as its label, so its gap gives 3.5 to 7px back to keep Trades to W equal to L to Accuracy (29/30px at 320, 35/36 at 390, 53/54 at 1280). **Sizing answer (same day; owner: always 100%, "all in into every single call"):** that is what the board already does (the whole balance on every call, compounding, no leverage); the bigger version is leverage: at 2x Claude +$79,303, Grok +$67,422, GPT +$26,224, Kimi −$61,525; at 5x (the real bet's SIM.leverage) all four lose: Grok −$1,929, Claude −$11,243 (low $5,863), GPT −$62,379, Kimi −$98,371 (no move reached 17.5%, so no wipe-out, but Accenture costs 80% at 5x). The card: Certificate, then "No. 0043 · Calls filed Oct 22" on one line (or Calls filed stacked at the right of What they predicted), the calls with PASS/FAIL and a pressed ink crown for the winner (the winner row in ink like the others), Market, "See the film" ("See the picture" on a still round) and the stamp. The ten: 1 the closing bell (a gold-foil bell, the EZ, AT THE CLOSE knocked out of a red band), 2 the postmark (a perforated EZ stamp cancelled by the market, the wavy lines following the round's move), 3 the warrant ("By appointment / to the market", the winner's crown in gold foil on a green and red roundel), 4 spot gloss (shiny green and red foil split along the Z's diagonal, the EZ cut out, a gold ring), 5 EZ Proof (a proof coin: mirror field, enamel EZ, gold), 6 the validation punch (the EZ cut through the card), 7 the code stamp (GRADED tab, green frame, gold date band), 8 the data seal (the ring is the first 64 bits of the SHA-256 of the round's calls and close), 9 the word stamp (EZ PROOF), 10 the advisory label (a black block, GRADED knocked out). Research (a helper, sources read 2026-10-25; the first two checked by me): colour is the hardest identity element to own and characters, logos and logotypes the easiest (Ward, Yang, Romaniuk and Beal 2020, 1,281 elements), and every trading app shares green and red, so the stamp should own a shape; a homemade seal beat every established SSL seal except Norton's in Baymard's 2023 test (looking official is cheap; the record behind it is the proof); more than two seals lowered conversion in field tests; certification marks keep an upright master, a one-colour version, clear space and a minimum size (ENERGY STAR, B Corp, USDA Organic; UL Verified pairs the mark with a unique ID and a verify address); say exactly what it certifies (Good Housekeeping and Nintendo both narrowed their seals' claims): the grading, not the AI; the Parental Advisory label had to be black and white "because it had to go on everything" and became a badge of honour (stamp the Rekt rounds too); conductors' punches were each a shape of their own; a proof finish is for the best strikes only (10s). Fixed on the way: a bare `<path id>` used for text on a path fills black (keep such paths in `<defs>`); tiny SVG type in a scaled box needs `text-rendering="geometricPrecision"` or Chrome spaces the letters unevenly ("G RADED"); a transform on the stamp's wrapper isolates it, so its ink cannot multiply into the paper (rotate the layers instead); the mockup label read "0.00% off on avg" on a round nobody called (the live site already says "0 of 4 right" alone). **Owner's verdict (same day):** none of the ten resonate; round 47's card is the layout ("the ones that I like in terms of the layout"); Calls filed belongs at the foot beside the stamp, never at the top (moving it to the number's line misread "positioned somewhere else": the owner meant away from See the film, a little higher, still at the foot).

Shapes: F film strip (three frames on a strip of film with lit sprocket holes, today brightest with an "On air" lightbox, the next frame dark; the bar as the film's edge print), B boarding pass (the route from R1 to NVDA "Bet #1" is the bar with a marker; three printed fields; the stub carries the tally lamp and "On air" turned up it), R receipt (a till roll with torn ends, a level meter, three lines with dotted leaders, the bar as a barcode of one bar per round, NVIDIA's in gold), S scorebug (a broadcast score graphic in the logo's black, the corner bug, the logo's green and red slashes at two corners, companies on cream discs). Live marks: lightbox (a studio sign, red glass lit on air, dark off air), tally (a lamp and the words), meter (four bars dancing on air, flat off air), bug (a slanted red TV tag with a blinking lamp); in the header pill each at most 109px at 390px. Flagged: Verizon's logo is a tick, so "on ✓ VZ" reads like a check mark.

- **Paper you can feel, live (owner, 2026-10-28, night: "please do that on web on mobile and push it to production"; live on earningszn.xyz, both artifacts and both previews):** x2plus.js PAPER (last block) and the PAPER block of skin-x2plus.css: every bet-stock ticket (`.bs-nx`: the strip under the film, the season ticket and tryouts stub, the proof, the bet and Season 2 tickets) gets a cut edge, a copy of its hard-shadow layer (`.bt-hs.bt-edge`, same die cut, kept in step with it by a MutationObserver on its style, offset forced to translate(.6px,2.4px) since the shadow's inline style carries its own offset) in a tan card gradient, and a raking light (`.bt-rake`, a 300%-wide gradient inside the cut paper `.bt-pp`, overlay-blended, moved by transform on a view timeline as the ticket scrolls past; static where view timelines are missing or motion is reduced), plus a slight bow on the paper (`.bt-p::after`, multiply). Scrolling at 4x slower CPU measured the same with and without it (about 48 fps, the same count of long frames, 3 runs each).

- **Forced dark mode (2026-10-24; owner: "everybody needs to have the same site regardless of their setup"):** a friend's Android phone showed the standings with the ink on every ticket cream on cream, the gold turned brown or dark red and the printed logos gone. That is a browser repainting a page it takes for a light one (Samsung Internet's dark mode; Chrome and in-app WebViews can do the same): text colours are lightened and flat colours darkened, while background images such as our paper texture are left alone. Reproduced the mechanism in Chromium (`--blink-settings=forceDarkModeEnabled=true` on a light control page: dark ink on a paper image turns light); our page itself stays correct there, because its root is dark, so the friend's browser decides differently (Samsung Internet up to 24 forces dark by default whatever the page says; from 25.0 its "Adaptive Force Dark" skips pages that declare their colour scheme; Samsung developer blog and forum). Fix, live on earningszn.xyz, both artifacts and both previews since 2026-10-24 (build.py, X2+ patch list): the page says dark, and only dark, everywhere it states its colours: X2's base sheet opened with `:root{color-scheme:light}` (now `dark` then `only dark`), the dark-theme rule and the inline styles the scripts set say `only dark`, plus a `prefers-color-scheme: dark` rule (older Samsung Internet looks for one); the meta tag stays `dark` (a browser that cannot read `only` in the meta would drop it). Pixel-identical in a normal browser (two live patches differ: the countdown's seconds and a loading ring). Not verified on the friend's phone; a browser that forces dark whatever the page says cannot be stopped from the page (Samsung's own reply on its forum), the visitor's fix there is Samsung Internet > Settings > Labs > "Use website dark theme", or dark mode off for the site.

It's just much more work per element but speed is trade off for quality"):** every element is drawn as its own hand-tuned shapes (irregular outlines, two-tone fills, shading, thick outer and thin inner ink), never a stock effect, a plain CSS box or one even pattern; painted layers come from Hedra (key `HEDRA_API_KEY`, v3 API, see `ez/hedra/REPORT.md`; only sessions started after 2026-10-31 12:20 UTC see it), and words and numbers stay page text, never baked into generated art. - **Films: reply "done" (owner, 2026-10-30: "No need to give me an output except done when I send you the videos"):** when the owner sends round films, check, encode and ship them as SYNC.md says, then reply only "done". Still flag a film that is held (a factual error) or anything out of order.
- **Ask, don't assume (owner, 2026-10-29, a standing rule, part of the modus operandi):** "whenever a single word I use could narrow the creative space or carries two meanings, you pause and ask: do you mean this or that? before acting. No word gets to quietly restrict the options; least-restrictive creativity is the default."
- **Close the gap before building (owner, 2026-10-25: "always ask yourself the question, what did the operator not ask that is actually really crucial... or you can just ask me follow-up questions so that the delta between my expectation and your interpretation converge... You can ask as many follow-up questions"):** before making a requested change, work out what the owner did not say but needs (who reads it, what must stay true as data changes, what the change implies elsewhere on the page) and ask follow-up questions freely, as many as it takes; do not guess where a question would settle it.
- **Carte blanche / "be creative" (owner, 2026-10-21):** when the owner asks for creative iterations or says "carte blanche", forget the existing look and earlier rounds; each option must be a genuinely different idea, not a re-skin of what is on the page. Holding back because "he may not want that much change" disappoints. Still keep the content rules (real data, guardrails) and the parts the owner said to keep.
- Site copy: no long dashes; plain, direct sentences. Do not use the "always up" benchmark (AIs losing to someone who buys every stock up) in new or current pages (owner, 2026-10-05).
- Titles (owner, 2026-10-08): hero and section heads are back in the site's own display type (Barlow Condensed, capitals); the newspaper serif tried on 2026-10-07 was reverted. Text under every head keeps one size (`--lede`, in the `<!--WSJ-->` block of `v15-season.html`, stripped from X1 by build.py).

- X2 Overview, the proof (owner, 2026-10-10): the switch is ROI (default, the Leaderboard's ROI chart) and Direction. How close and the "$100K on each AI, now worth" list are off the X2 Overview (How close stays in the Leaderboard stats as Avg miss). EZ Season keeps Direction, How close and the list.
- Design work (layouts, new pages, visuals): research current sources first (web search: usability studies, comparable products, recent practice), cite them, and critique the first draft before shipping. Do not design from memory alone; if research is skipped, say why (owner, 2026-10-06).
